Abstract: Guaranteeing performance for frequency control is challenging within traditional control paradigms, as it demands precise disturbance estimation and real-time control signal computation within a timeframe of seconds. This paper addresses this challenge by proposing a stability-certified imitation learning framework for frequency regulation in low-inertia microgrids. Based on the frequency response model of the microgrid, an offline numerical optimal control is employed to generate near-optimal pairs of state and control dataset, which are subsequently used to train a deep neural network (DNN) feedback controller. To ensure the closed-loop stability, the DNN controller is trained with a set of Lyapunov-based stability constraints derived using absolute stability theory. Such an imitation learning paradigm can significantly improve the training efficiency, while the resulting DNN controller acts as an end-to-end function that simultaneously handles disturbance estimation and control optimization. The proposed framework is validated on a modified IEEE 33-node diesel-wind microgrid. The DNN controllers are deployed in wind turbine generators (WTGs) to provide decentralized, intelligent supplementary active power support to always maintain frequency deviations within predefined safety bounds under different disturbances while preserving closed-loop stability.

</details>

RMS and EMT values differ in network, plant model, controls, operating point, and base, and so they are reported as separate, unaligned observations. The paper reports the errors the authors identified in the agent logs, 63 author messages, and 3.8 days from agent start to the integrated study report. The agents executed and documented the predefined ride-through and stability workflow, which covers one part of an interconnection study.

Abstract: Learning-based surrogates for alternating-current optimal power flow (AC-OPF) are often judged by the gap between their objective value and that of a nonlinear solver, which does not show that a prediction satisfies power balance and network limits. This paper tests whether predictions from the pretrained foundation model GridSFM-Open 1.1.0, recently released by Microsoft, are feasible AC-OPF operating points and whether they are useful initial values for a nonlinear solver. The model was evaluated zero-shot and after fine-tuning on 10, 100, and 1,000 labeled scenarios (FT10, FT100, and FT1000) on 500 scenarios for each of two synthetic grids, ACTIVSg2000 (Texas) and ACTIVSg500 (South Carolina), with Ipopt, MATPOWER MIPS, and pandapower PIPS as solver references. The lowest Texas median cost gap, 0.44\% for FT100, coincided with thermal violations in 24.1\% of cases and a median maximum bus power mismatch of 89 MW and 197 Mvar. Texas zero-shot predictions had a median maximum mismatch of 648 MW and 1,641 Mvar, and every South Carolina case violated a thermal limit for every model variant. Seeding Ipopt with FT1000 predictions gave geometric-mean solve-time speedups of 1.55x on South Carolina and 1.12x on Texas (p = 0.06, not significant at the 5\% level), while zero-shot seeds slowed South Carolina solves (0.26x). A small objective gap therefore did not indicate feasibility: although median zero-shot inference took only 1.32 s on Texas and 0.157 s on South Carolina on a shared host, every prediction still required a nonlinear solver, and fine-tuned predictions gave modest, grid-dependent warm-start speedups while zero-shot predictions slowed convergence on one grid. GridSFM may therefore be useful for initializing a nonlinear solver, but obtaining feasible AC-OPF solutions still required the solver.

</details>

Abstract: Dynamic wireless charging (DWC) is an emerging technology that enables electric vehicles (EVs) to recharge while traveling. Its unique operational model creates two revenue streams for DWC operators through lane access tolls and electricity sales. However, these prices influence not only traffic flow and roadway energy delivery but also how much EV charging moves to homes instead, potentially within the same distribution network. Neglecting this coupling can underestimate grid loading and compromise pricing decisions. We develop a coupled traffic, EV energy, driver-response, and AC distribution network model with a model predictive control (MPC) framework that jointly optimizes DWC tolls and charging prices while explicitly accounting for displaced residential charging and network losses. Using 78 days of I-210 West traffic data, we show that conventional MPC exceeds feeder loading limits on 52 days, whereas the proposed residential load aware controller eliminates these violations with only 0.6% lower operator revenue. Under a more constrained feeder configuration, it eliminates 50 overloaded days with a 0.9% revenue reduction. Sensitivity analyses highlight the importance of accurate residential demand estimation and feeder loss modeling, while peak-aware pricing further reduces daily peak loading by 3.7% relative to the base load. These results demonstrate the value of coordinated pricing that captures the interaction between roadway and residential charging.

</details>

Abstract: This paper proposes the Multi-Agent Distributional Wide-Margin Policy Gradient (MADWMPG) framework: a dual-policy hybrid imitation and safe reinforcement learning (IL-SRL) method designed for safely mitigating grid imbalance in multiphase active distribution networks with distributed soft open points (SOPs). The framework comprises a wide-margin constraint barrier recourse (WCBR) module that seeks the maximal separation of constraint-compliant and unsafe actions and learns to proactively suppress exploratory violations. The framework addresses operational volatility using a novel adaptation of noncrossing quantile regression (NQR) distributional critics that track the complete distribution of policy returns. The set of behavior and recourse policies integrates an instantaneous physics-based and differentiable action projection mechanism that generates SOP constraint-compliant actions without relying on active learning. Policy safety is made responsive using an enhancement of conventional experience replay, and the safety performance of the framework is supported by probabilistic bounds derived in the paper. The proposed framework was trained and tested on a modified multiphase IEEE 123-bus test feeder. Results demonstrate its superiority to conventional reinforcement learning and SRL methods and show that it is highly competitive against the nonlinear AC optimal power flow.

Abstract: AI data centers receive computing tasks continuously, whereas solver-based optimization takes time to run and electricity is priced per settlement period. This paper bridges this timing gap through a solver-free online optimization rule inspired by dual decomposition. The rule assigns each task a start period upon arrival to optimize energy cost subject to operational constraints, shifting flexible tasks toward low-price periods while keeping high-priority tasks on schedule. Simulations on the A100 GPU trace (3,456 GPUs, 1,343 tasks) show that the proposed rule keeps GPU and CPU use within capacity while scheduling tasks at low cost. Compared with a multi-timescale optimization benchmark, it achieves a lower cost and, because it needs no solver, much less computation time.

</details>

Abstract: This paper studies coordinated Large Language Model (LLM) training-task scheduling and facility operation for geographically distributed cloud data centers (CDCs). The central question is how two scheduling flexibilities - cross-CDC task transfer and task reordering - affect service and economic outcomes when remote execution remains subject to compute, bandwidth, energy, and zone-temperature constraints. We formulate a mixed-integer optimization model that jointly determines task outcomes, heterogeneous-node assignment, execution timing, pre-execution data transfer, node activation, and facility operation. A deterministic 24-hour case study contains 3 CDCs, 15 heterogeneous graphics processing unit (GPU) nodes, 4 thermal zones per CDC, and 103 tasks. In this instance, enabling transfer increases net profit by 57.9% relative to the otherwise matched no-transfer variant and increases completed tasks from 76 to 103. The more restrictive no-transfer FIFO-prefix variant completes 14 tasks and yields negative profit. The proposed schedule transfers 39 tasks, achieves 70.3% node utilization, and maintains zone temperatures within 24°C-27°C. These fixed-instance results indicate that cross-CDC transfer and task-reordering flexibility can improve the modeled profit and task-completion outcomes under the studied conditions.

</details>

Abstract: This paper explores the connections between the criteria directly or indirectly minimized by Prediction Error (PE), Instrumental Variable (IV), and Pseudo-Linear Regression (PLR) algorithms for the identification of output-error models. By definition, PE methods aim to minimize the variance of the prediction error. In contrast, the correlation functions appearing in the stationarity conditions of IV and PLR algorithms are not the gradients of any underlying loss function involving a signal variance. Nevertheless, if the true system lies in the model set, we show that one IV algorithm minimizes asymptotically the variance of an unmeasurable signal called the equivalent prediction error. This signal was introduced previously to analyze the bias distribution of PLR schemes. It turns out that, regardless of whether the true system lies in the model set, some iterative IV and PLR algorithms aim to minimize the prediction error variance and can therefore be interpreted as indirect PE algorithms.

</details>

Abstract: In this work, we present a fully sampling-based explicit dual control algorithm that combines a particle filter and model predictive path integral control for partially observed nonlinear systems. The key idea is to roll out multiple sampled input sequences from all particles of the particle filter to obtain a belief-averaged control cost that induces caution. The same rollouts are used to sample imagined future measurements, which are drawn from the predictive distribution of the particle belief. This results in a Monte Carlo information term that gives a sample estimate of the expected information gain of each sampled input sequence and thereby induces probing. In contrast to existing explicit dual control schemes, the proposed scheme neither relies on gradients of the dynamics or measurement models nor requires a Gaussian belief. Furthermore, all computations can be parallelized across input sequences, particles, and imagined measurements, which makes the scheme amenable to real-time implementation on suitable hardware. We demonstrate the effectiveness of the proposed method in a simulation example of a planar terrain-aided navigation task.

</details>

Abstract: Accurate prediction of thermo-mechanical wafer deformation is essential for sub-nanometer overlay in extreme ultraviolet (EUV) lithography. Localized heating expands the wafer, while friction between the wafer and its supporting elastic pillars causes hysteresis. Modeling this is challenging because of the system's large scale, the computational cost of friction-induced hysteresis and the sparse deformation measurements. This work augments a linear thermo-mechanical baseline model with a learning-based structure in a linear fractional representation (LFR) framework, identified from sparse measurements. Simulation results show that the augmented model captures the unmodeled dynamics with hysteretic effects and predicts the deformation significantly more accurately than the baseline.

</details>

Abstract: Accurate and robust estimation of battery state-of-health (SOH) and remaining useful life (RUL) is essential for safe and cost-effective operation of electrified transportation systems, yet it remains challenging under operating variability, non-stationary aging, and non-Gaussian measurement disturbances. This paper proposes Multi-scale Adaptive Robust State Estimation (MARS), a multi-scale adaptive robust state-estimation framework that couples a dual-branch temporal encoder with an equivalent-circuit-model-constrained differentiable generalized-correntropy adaptive Kalman filter. The encoder disentangles fast intra-cycle voltage-current-temperature dynamics from slow inter-cycle degradation, and the fused degradation context adaptively parameterizes the cycle-level process covariance, measurement covariance, and correntropy bandwidth. Through the unrolled robust filtering recursion, MARS performs physics-consistent state propagation and generalized-correntropy-based innovation correction, while uncertainty decomposition and Monte Carlo dropout provide calibrated prediction intervals for SOH estimation and conditional RUL prediction. Experiments on the NASA and CALCE benchmarks demonstrate improved SOH accuracy, achieving mean absolute error/root mean square error values of 0.72%/0.92% and 0.63%/0.80%, respectively, together with competitive conditional RUL performance against representative data-driven and hybrid baselines. The resulting conditional-RUL prediction intervals achieve coverage probabilities of 100.00% and 99.24% on NASA and CALCE, respectively. Ablation, disturbance-sensitivity, near-end-of-life, and runtime analyses further verify the contribution of each module and the practical inference cost for online battery management.

</details>

Abstract: Galvanically isolated gate drivers are required in many power electronics applications. This paper describes an implementation of a digital isolator using discrete components with a planar coreless PCB signal transformer providing the isolation. The use of an electrostatic shield between the transformer windings is shown to provide sufficient common-mode transient immunity to support IGBT half-bridge drivers, an application subject to a severe dV/dt transient during switching. The design is verified through the experimental implementation of a prototype half bridge using two of the proposed discrete digital isolators with SiC power transistors and demonstrates common-mode transient immunity greater than 15 kV/us with very low implementation complexity.

### [436] [Classical heat exchanger fouling-resistance laws as first-moment identities of additive stochastic deposition](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.22808)
*Simone Casolo*

Main category: eess.SY

TL;DR: 解析错误


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: 解析错误

Method: 解析错误

Result: 解析错误

Conclusion: 解析错误

Abstract: Heat-exchanger fouling is monitored through the fouling resistance, whose record is routinely fitted with the classical linear (Ebert--Panchal) or asymptotic (Kern--Seaton) law, and the shape of the fit is often taken as evidence of the underlying deposition or removal mechanism. We show that it cannot be. If the deposit grows by non-negative, statistically stationary increments, the mean fouling resistance is exactly linear without removal and exactly the Kern--Seaton asymptote under first-order removal, whatever the microscopic deposition law. Shear-driven erosion, per-element detachment, intermittent sloughing and saturation of the surface all give the same mean curve, so a fit identifies a net deposition rate and a relaxation time, and nothing else. Modelling the deposit as a Levy subordinator, we show where the missing information lies and what it costs to retrieve it from monitoring data. Removal mechanisms separate in the variance: sloughing leaves a plateau scatter that persists even for smooth deposition and can be read from a record that saturates. Deposition laws separate only in higher cumulants, which the measurement noise of a thermally reconstructed resistance attenuates; the record needed to distinguish them grows as the fourth power of the conductance error, which places it beyond reach at realistic measurement accuracy. For the time to reach a cleaning limit, the deposition law matters only near the threshold; farther away, the mean rate and its uncertainty dominate the prediction.

</details>

Abstract: Data centres that offer the same reduction in grid demand can make different contributions to supply adequacy. We estimate the effective load-carrying capability of workload scheduling and on-site batteries by matching expected unserved energy across paired simulations. Longer task deadlines increase capacity credit by giving operators more choice over when to defer and recover work. A fixed evening commitment shifts more work than deadline-based scheduling but delivers a smaller reduction in expected unserved energy. Protected backup reserves leave uninterruptible power supplies with limited capacity credit, while longer discharge durations raise the credit of dedicated batteries at the same response rating. These findings suggest assessing recovery deadlines and dispatchable energy alongside offered demand reductions.

Abstract: In this paper, we analyze stability and recursive feasibility of a model predictive control (MPC) scheme that does not rely on terminal ingredients -- such as terminal constraints or a terminal cost based on a terminal controller. In the proposed formulation, we augment the cost function by a weighted finite tail which is optimized online, and we show it plays the role of a stabilizing terminal cost. Under a relaxed, local dynamic programming condition on the tail, we certify a family of regions -- parameterized by the tail's weight coefficient -- on which the problem is recursively feasible and the origin is asymptotically stable. These regions are larger as the weight increases: in particular, we show that any state that can be steered within the control horizon to a predefined neighborhood of the origin is contained in a certified region for a sufficiently large weight. Finally, we characterize the closed-loop performance over the certified region, bounding the suboptimality of the closed loop with respect to the infinite-horizon optimal cost. We conclude the discussion by illustrating these results on a simple numerical example.

</details>

Abstract: Signal Temporal Logic is a popular framework for describing a robotic system's goals. Once described using a temporal logic framework, synthesis turns the goals into actual steps for the robots to follow. Currently, the state of the art for sound and complete synthesis is using Mixed-Integer Linear Programming (MILP), which struggles on long time horizons and large environments, preventing the construction of guaranteed safe plans for long deployments. This paper introduces a new synthesis method using Satisfiability Modulo Theories (SMT) which scales better for long deployments and adapts to environmental changes more quickly. We do this by introducing an encoding method for STL into SMT constraints, and an encoding method for discrete-time dynamical systems modeled as a transition system. We then conduct an empirical comparison of our SMT based methods against the state of the art MILP based methods. Results show that for this class of problems, SMT is an effective encoding strategy that performs the best on long time horizon tasks.

</details>

Result: 解析错误

Abstract: MRI is not designed to operate in the presence of EMI emitting electronic devices. To accomodate this, we propose VERGE which improves on previous EMI removal methods and can operate without external EMI sensors. VERGE estimates a reference coil non-linearly from all imaging and non-imaging coils by training a small per-scan convolutional neural net to generate a virtual coil. The virtual coil is then used with STRIDE, a reference-based EMI removal method. For single-source EMI contamination, VERGE performed similarly to the reference method. For multi-source dynamic EMI contamination, VERGE outperformed the comparison methods. Notably, VERGE with zero physical EMI sensors outperformed the comparison methods even with two physical EMI sensors. A non-linearly estimated virtual EMI coil improves reference-coil-based EMI removal under dynamic multi-source EMI, and enables effective EMI removal without physical EMI sensors or training data.

</details>


### [441] [Uncovering the Invisible in Routine Radiology: Cross-Modal Detection of MRI-Verified Soft-Tissue Pathologies from CBCT via Physics-Guided 3D Self-Supervised Learning](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.23042)
*Evgeny Alves Limarenko*

Main category: eess.IV

TL;DR: 解析错误


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: 解析错误

Method: 解析错误

Result: 解析错误

Conclusion: 解析错误

Abstract: Routine X-ray modalities like cone-beam computed tomography (CBCT) are optimized for high-contrast mineralized tissues, leaving soft-tissue abnormalities buried beneath photon noise and below human visual perception. Thus, detecting non-calcified pathologies requires secondary magnetic resonance imaging (MRI). We demonstrate that sub-visual radiographic signatures of soft-tissue pathology are physically preserved within routine CBCT scans and can be extracted for opportunistic screening using a targeted, physics-guided deep learning pipeline, even under extreme paired-data scarcity. Using the temporomandibular joint (TMJ) as a model system (N = 212 paired CBCT-MRI joints), we address the severe overfitting of high-capacity 3D networks through a two-stage framework. First, we employ a frozen full-grid 3D masked autoencoder (BertsWin) pre-trained on 6,550 unlabeled CBCT volumes via the GradientConductor (GCond) optimizer and an anatomy-aware objective (PhysLoss) redirecting gradient flow into the [-300, 300] HU soft-tissue window. Second, automated localization of the condylar zenith drives a deterministic 3D cropping that eliminates 81% of background osseous variance. Evaluated via a patient-isolated 3x10-fold stratified cross-validation protocol, a statistically robust sub-visual signal for MRI-verified synovial effusion (ROC AUC = 0.793) emerges exclusively from representations pre-trained with the PhysLoss and GCond regime. Crucially, only this setup captures true 3D anatomical chirality (p < 0.001) and high intra-patient structural consistency (0.791) over inter-patient noise; conversely, identical architectures pre-trained with standard L2 loss or unmasked variance decomposition (MVC) collapse to random guessing. These findings establish a generalizable paradigm for repurposing routine, low-contrast radiographic scans into opportunistic screening tools for occult soft-tissue disease.

</details>
